### Internal contradictions in the book, kept side by side

- **The East India Company's first factory: "(a) 1635-62" (p. 24, printed 15) against "from 1636 to
  1662" (p. 28, printed 19). "Do not resolve the 1635/1636 discrepancy — it is the book's, not a
  transcription fault, both being printed cleanly."** The second factory agrees at 1758–75.
- **Nadir Shah's invasion dated three times:** "in 1737" (p. 32, printed 23), "in 1739" (p. 35,
  printed 26), "however, in 1739" (p. 38, printed 29). **"Two dates, both printed cleanly, twenty
  pages apart."**
- **Withington's journey, 1613 against 1614, now attested across two chapters:** "the first of March
  1614" (p. 80, folio 71) against "the guards paid by Withington in 1613" (p. 91, folio 82), "the
  route followed by Withington in 1613" (p. 98, folio 89) and "the days of Withing­ton (1613)"
  (p. 169, folio 160, footnote 2). **"The disagreement is now attested across two chapters and should
  be settled from the PDF at consolidation."**
- **Who took Shikarpur from the Daudpotras:** Yar Muhammad "succeeded in wresting `Shikarpiir` from
  the `Daiidpotras`" about 1701, and a few lines later his son Nur Muhammad "increased his father's
  dominion by adding the `Shikarpiir` country of the `Daiidpotras`" (p. 38, printed 29). **"The chunk
  does not explain how it was taken twice."**
- **The Shahbunder house within the same year's letters:** "but just habitable", would "prove a
  graveyard", warehouses "very improper to lodge valuable goods in" — and immediately below, "in
  pretty good condition… The warehouses are quite dry and free from damp" (p. 70, folio 61).
- **Three witnesses on when Sind became tributary** (pp. 61–63, 66; folios 52–54, 57): Callendar's
  account, Erskine's 1740/"about 1757", and the Bombay Castle letter of 8 April 1758. **"reported as
  they stand."**
- **Famine absent and famine present, fifteen pages apart:** "famines … had ordinarily no terrors for
  the Sindhi peasant" (p. 129, folio 120) and Hamilton's "' they never know the misery of famine '"
  (p. 128, folio 119) against "80,000 weavers had died of plague, caused by the · failure of rains"
  before 1699 (pp. 126–127, folios 117–118). **"Sorley never addresses the contradiction; the word
  'ordinarily' is the only hedge."**
- **Carless dated 1837 (p. 132, folio 123) and 1836 (p. 134, folio 125) "for what reads as the same
  observation"**, with a third Carless citation carrying no year at all (p. 133, folio 124).
- **Aurangzeb's Multan governorship dated twice, differently in form:** "(1648-52)" (p. 143,
  folio 134) against "became Governor of Multan in 1648 and in November 1649 the sarkar of Tatta was
  added to his viceroyalty" (p. 151, folio 142) — **"The chunk gives no end date in the second
  passage."** Appendix V adds `1648-5 1[text-layer?]` (p. 200, folio 191).
- **The poet's death and his tomb:** death "1752" (pp. 172, 179; folio 170) against the tomb "built …
  in 1754" and "later in 1 754[text-layer?]" (p. 183, folio 174). **"Not necessarily a contradiction —
  a tomb can postdate a burial — but the chunk never says where the body lay in the interval — (not
  stated)."**
- **Ghulam Shah Kalhoro pays for the tomb in 1754 but only becomes paramount in 1757–8** (p. 183,
  folio 174 against p. 201, folio 192). **"any entry saying 'the ruler Ghulam Shah Kalhoro built the
  tomb' would be overstating what the book supports."**
- **The Durrani date against the poet's stated age:** "He was fifty-eight when Ahmad Shah Durani
  brought off his coup" (p. 180, folio 171), which against the birth year 1689 gives 1747, against
  Appendix V's `1748` and `1 750[text-layer?]` (p. 201, folio 192). **"One year out against the
  first, three out against the second. The other three age-anchored events (1707, 1739, 1758)
  reconcile exactly. Record the mismatch; do not resolve it."**
- **Appendix V drops the hedge the narrative insists on** — 1689 and 1752 printed flat (p. 201,
  folio 192) against the narrative's twice-stated "not established with complete certainty".
- **The Sumra are called Hindu, Muslim and Rajput within twelve lines** (p. 174, folio 165), and the
  book adds two further characterisations at p. 247 (folio 238) — **"Five accounts across two chunks,
  none reconciled by the book."** Separately the Sumra chronology contains four statements that do
  not agree, all on p. 270 (folio 261): an 18-year reign circa A.D. 1228; the *Ain-i-Akbari*'s
  thirty-six princes over five hundred years; the *Tarikh-i-Tahiri*'s Hijri `7 00[text-layer?]` to
  843; and Elliot on the eleventh-century Qarmatian taint. **"Hijri 700–843 is a span of 143 years,
  not five hundred; the chunk states both without comment."**
- **Two datings of mass conversion a page apart** (pp. 174–175, folios 165–166) and a third window in
  chunk 016 (p. 248, folio 239): twelfth century onwards, complete by the sixteenth. **"Overlapping
  but not identical windows; kept side by side."**
- **Which cities Mahmud of Ghazni found Ismaili-held:** "both Multan and Mansiirah[text-layer?]"
  (p. 174, folio 165, quoting Ray) against "Mansura" alone in Sorley's own words (p. 248, folio 239).
- **The poet's education:** "It is quite certain that Shah Abdul Latif was not a . lea:rned[text-layer?]
  mari[text-layer?], _ in the narrow scholastic sense" (p. 224, folio 215) against "His poems show
  clearly an acquaintance with Arabic and Persian far beyond the ordinary accomplishments of his
  time" and "his education was neither superficial nor contemptible" (p. 183, folio 174). **"the book
  does not reconcile them itself."**
- **Whether he had a teacher:** "a Niir Muhammad Bhatti", "One of his preceptors" (p. 212, folio 203)
  against the popular tradition "that he had no regular education but taught himself everything",
  which Sorley calls "unreliable" and for which he names no teacher (p. 183, folio 174).
- **Whose verses are embedded in the Risalo:** Shah Karim's "few of his verses" (p. 212, folio 203)
  against Inayat's, "some of whose verses are inserted or quoted in the Risa.Io[text-layer?] as it
  exists today" (p. 184, folio 175). **"the book never brings them together."**
- **Whether Islam in Sind had a hierarchy:** "Islam has never had the established hierarchy of
  Christian medievalism" (p. 214, folio 205) against the poet being ranged against "the jealousy of
  the orthodox ' hierarchy '" (p. 182, folio 173). **"The word 'hierarchy' is doing opposite work."**
- **How far the poet travelled:** "he had travelled wid-ely[text-layer?] for his day" (p. 255,
  folio 246) against "It is true that he did not travel far" (pp. 181–182, folios 172–173).
- **Kotri as home and Kotri as neighbour:** "he resided successively in villages called Hala Haweli,
  Kotri and Bhit Shah" (p. 180, folio 171) against "the Kohistan and Kotri localities not far from
  his own residence" (p. 255, folio 246).
- **What was constantly in his hands:** three books — Koran, Rumi's Masnawi, Shah Karim's Sindhi
  verses (p. 183, folio 174) — against one, hedged: "a copy of whose ' Masnawi ' was said to be
  constantly in his hands" (p. 251, folio 242).
- **Kalhora power over the Baluch**, unreconciled within one chunk: pp. 163 (folio 154), 169
  (folio 160) and 171 (folio 162).
- **The cost of justice:** Burnes's "The administration of justice costs little or nothing to the
  state" (p. 162, folio 153) against the *Amini* impost of "one-fourth of the sums disputed"
  (p. 158, folio 149) and prisoners kept on public alms with the surplus to the kotwal (p. 160,
  folio 151). **"Cheap to the treasury, not cheap to the litigant; the book does not draw the
  distinction."**
- **Music debased and music revered**, on facing pages (pp. 228–229, folios 219–220); **the minstrel
  class as preserver and as captor of the art** (p. 227, folio 218); **Sorley dismissing the musical
  question as irrelevant and building a chapter on it** (p. 231, folio 222 against p. 229, folio 220).
- **The Risalo as poor evidence and as accurate portrayal:** "the Risalo is not a treasure house of
  graphic detail", "There is surprisingly little of Sind topography in the Risalo" (pp. 265–266,
  folios 256–257) against "No one familiar · with Sind will dispute how true a portrayal this is"
  (pp. 267–268, folios 258–259). **"Any entry drawing rural or economic detail from this chapter must
  carry both statements."**
- **Sufism in Sind as a coherent system and as no consistent progress, on the same page** (p. 302,
  folio 293), with "Siifism[text-layer?] in Sind has been handed down by a handful of learned men
  from age to age" (p. 303, folio 294). **"Kept side by side; not reconciled."**
- **The "living Pir" epithet is given by this book to two different saints and the book never
  notices** — Khwajo Khizr (p. 178, folio 169; pp. 301–302, folios 292–293) and Lal Shahbaz /
  "Jivelal" (p. 257, folio 248). **"two of the book's three passages assign the epithet to Khwajo
  Khizr and only chunk 016's assigns it to Lal Shahbaz. That is evidence, not proof."**
- **The glossary contradicts itself about who Suhini's lover is and whether Mehar is Punhun**
  (pp. 430–431, folios 421–422): "Sihir[text-layer?]  Name of Suhini's lover ; ' Helper ' .";
  "Dim[text-layer?]  Name of Suhini's husband."; "Hot … Used by Suhini of Punhun her lover.";
  "Mehir[text-layer?]  Buffalo keeper. One of the names given to Punhiin[text-layer?].";
  "Punhiin[text-layer?]  Na·me[text-layer?] of Sasui's lover." **"Four entries, at least two of which
  cannot all be right. The archive must not use the glossary to settle who belongs to which tale."**
- **The index against the book's own running text:** `Murad Buksh Shah . (See Lal Shahbaz)` (p. 438)
  against the same index's `Sayid Muhammad Husain (Pir Murad) 248, 291` (p. 439) and against the
  running text at p. 257 (folio 248), where Pir Murad's tomb is at Tatta and Lal Shahbaz is a
  different saint at Sehwan. **"The index therefore sends one of Pir Murad's three name-forms to the
  wrong saint, or the running text is wrong, or the two figures are conflated somewhere in the book."**
- **Internal tensions inside the verse itself, to be printed rather than resolved:** the poems both
  forbid and perform petition at a door (p. 321, folio 312 against pp. 330, 335, 339, 341); "the
  raingod" and "Allah" address the same weather (pp. 343, 344, 347); the search is interior and the
  journey pointless (sections XIV and the p. 380 sequence) against "Who go to Windar, let them gird
  their loins" (sections XVIII, XV, XXXI, XXXIX); and, four lines apart on one page, "My husband's
  kin / Bore me ill:-will." against "No ! No ! not wicked \vere[text-layer?] / My husband's kin."
  (p. 385, folio 376).
- **The book's own structure stated two ways:** "In Three Books : I. History / II. Literature and
  Criticism / III. The Risalo of Shah Abdul Latif" (p. 4) and the body head `BOOK I / HISTORY`
  (pp. 10, 12), against the Preface's twice-repeated "Part II" and "Part I" (p. 8) and the contents'
  "INDEX TO PARTS J [text-layer?] AND II" (p. 13). **"Reported as printed; not reconciled."**

### The book against the sources it quotes

- **The Manrique "Sind leather" passage is printed twice in two different wordings and "the chunk
  gives no sign that Sorley noticed"** (p. 85, folio 76 against p. 108, folio 99): "Sinde" vs "Sind",
  "back stitch work" vs "back-stitched work", "lined and furnished off" vs "finished off", "in the
  hot weather" vs "in summer" — and only the second is footnoted "Manrique : op. cit. , p. 239."
  **"Whether this is Sorley re-translating, two different editions, or text-layer damage is (not
  stated). A human must not quote one as the book's wording without noting the other."**
- **Two versions of one Dames quotation, differing by a preposition:** "let it fl.oat[text-layer?]
  **into** the Khwaja's waves" (p. 301, folio 292) against "let it float **in** the
  Khwaj a's[text-layer?] waves" (p. 256, folio 247), same source, same page 73 of Dames. **"One of the
  two text-layer readings is wrong and the chunk cannot say which."**
- **"bright array" against "fine array" in the same six-line stanza printed twice (p. 243 against
  p. 345) — "the most important single thing a human must check in this file. Neither word carries a
  `[text-layer?]` flag, so this is not OCR noise on its face."** The p. 345 printing also differs in
  punctuation throughout and prints "donned foday[text-layer?]" where p. 243 prints "donned today".
- **Four verses printed earlier with sur titles are printed again without any title in the
  translation, and no equation is made** (chunk 021): the dove verse (p. 352, folio 343 against
  p. 243, folio 234, "Sur Kedaro"); the King/Minstrel dialogue (p. 355, folio 346 against p. 250,
  folio 241, "Sur Sorath", **and "chunk 016 printed it as one continuous speech while this chunk
  distributes it between `King.`, `Minstrel.` and `Sayid.` labels"**); Sasui poem I stanzas four and
  five (p. 371, folio 362 against p. 254, folio 245, "Sur Rip"); and **the sharpest case, the "Sur
  Dahir" opening (p. 372, folio 363 against p. 254, folio 245), where "the continuation differs
  completely"**. Chunk 022 adds a fourth stanza at p. 373 (folio 364) that the p. 254 printing does
  not carry. **"either the commentary chapter's sur attributions and the translation section's
  story-cycle arrangement are two different ways of indexing the same poems, or the book is
  inconsistent."**
- **Sorley uses Burton as an authority he has just rejected** — Burton's verdict refused at p. 143
  (folio 134) while Burton supplies the canal-clearance detail at p. 141 (folio 132) and the whole
  intoxicants section at p. 147 (folio 138). **"The book does not flag the tension."**
- **Sorley declares the *Ain-i-Akbari* statistics worthless for Sind (p. 97, folio 88) and then
  quotes the *Ain* twice** (p. 97 footnote; p. 109, folio 100), bracketing only the first as
  exaggeration; and **accepts Postans' nineteenth-century trade lists as "accurate enough for the
  seventeenth and eighteenth centuries" (p. 100, folio 91) while devoting the surrounding pages to
  how completely the trade had changed. "Anything taken from those lists is a back-projection the
  book makes openly."**
- **"Sorley also disqualifies every source he then uses" — five disqualifications in two pages**
  (pp. 298–299, folios 289–290): "There is no reliable account of the progress of the movement in
  Simi[text-layer?]"; "this kind of information is useless"; "Nor is any of the modern day Sindhi
  work on this subject in better case"; "the facts are meagre"; "there is very little that can be.
  said to be securely established". **"the Sehwan ʿurs account, the Tatta dance account and the Lal
  Shahbaz biography all rest on the sources so disqualified. Any archive entry drawing on them must
  carry the caveat."**
- **Nicholson quoted on both sides of the Indian-origins question** (p. 286, folio 277 against
  p. 288, folio 279), and his "four principal foreign sources" list printed with only three commas —
  "Christianity, Neoplatonism Gnosticism, and Indian asceticism" (p. 284, folio 275). **"the printed
  list reads as three items where the text says four… not repaired here."**
- **An apparent duplication in the book itself, not a transcription fault:** the Muscat agent's two
  vessels at Karachi and an identical clause printed twice, once as a block quotation (p. 67) and
  again as running text (pp. 68–69), with "Karachi" in one and "Carrachee" in the other.
- **A duplicated clause in the Del Hoste quotation** (p. 168, folio 159): "I will stay here all day"
  printed twice with a stray semicolon. **"Whether this is a compositor's dittograph, a text-layer
  duplication, or Del Hoste's own rendering of repeated haranguing is (not stated)."**
- **The "Datah" canal (p. 198, folio 189) and chunk 012's "Dato canal" — "probably the same
  watercourse, and this file does not say they are… If a human confirms it, the archive gains a
  measured canal for an otherwise unlocated shrine site."**
- **"Crookes" corrected by the bibliography in two different directions** (p. 177, folio 168 against
  p. 193, folio 184).
- **Lane-Poole's Aurangzeb passage has lost its quotation marks** (p. 17): `Carry this creature u of
  dust to the nearest burial place`, with a stray `u` for an opening mark and the closing mark
  stranded on its own line. **"The exact wording of Aurangzeb's reported command should be verified
  before quoting."**

### Placement doubts: one name, two or more archive rows

- **"Inayat", raised independently by five chunks and resolved by none** — p. 184 (folio 175), p. 233
  (folio 224), p. 334 (folio 325), p. 367 (folio 358), p. 377 (folio 368), plus the glossary
  "Iniyat[text-layer?]  Name of a Sindhi poet." (p. 430, folio 421) and the index "Inayat,
  1 75[text-layer?], 224" (p. 435). Candidates: **`dargah-roza-sufi-shah-inayat-shaheed`** (Jhok
  Sharif, d. 1718), **`shrine-of-shah-inayat-qadiri`** (Lahore, c. 1728) and, on name alone,
  **`ganj-e-inayat-sarkar`** (Lahore, d. 2011). **"A third possibility the chunk leaves open is that
  'Inayat' is neither, but a signature the poet or a compiler used, exactly as 'Sayid', 'Lakhmir' and
  'Junej6[text-layer?]' stand in the same position."** No place, no date, no order, no shrine —
  **(not stated)**. **This must be resolved by a human, not by a worker.**
- **"Khwajo Khizr" / "Zinda Pir" at Sukkur, open for four chunks running** (p. 178, folio 169;
  pp. 301–302, folios 292–293; p. 441). **`gurdwara-pehli-patshahi-jind-pir-sukkur`** ("Near the
  Sukkur–Rohri (Lansdowne/Ayub) railway bridge" — the island site) and
  **`darbar-ghamkol-sharif-zinda-pir`** (Kohat, Khwaja Muhammad Qasim, d. 1999). **"The chunk's Zinda
  Pir is a river saint at Sukkur with a tenth-century mosque, which cannot be the Kohat shrine on any
  reading of the text; but the chunk never uses the word 'Jind Pir', never mentions a gurdwara, Guru
  Nanak or Nanakpanthi custodianship, and calls the site a 'mosque'."** Chunk 019 adds the sect "The
  Darya­panthis[text-layer?]" (p. 301, folio 292), which brings `darya-lal-mandir-darya-lal-sankat-mochan-mandir`,
  `jhollay-lal-mandir` and `shrine-at-odero-lal-udero-lal-teerath-asthan` into range. **Not merged.**
  The index's own equation `Zinda Pir (Khwajo Khizr) 169, 292` (p. 441) is **"the Indus personified"**.
- **The Udero Lal / Lal Shahbaz equation — the book makes it twice and the archive does not make it
  at all.** "the shrine of the Kalandar Shah Lal Baz (or Udero Lal) at Sehwan" (p. 168, folio 159)
  and the index's `Udero Lal (see Lal Shahbaz) 159` (p. 441). The archive carries
  **`lal-shahbaz-qalandar`** (Sehwan Sharif, Jamshoro) and **`shrine-at-odero-lal-udero-lal-teerath-asthan`**
  (Odero Lal village, Matiari District, Sheikh Tahir / Udero Lal (Jhulelal)) as **two separate rows
  at two separate places**, plus `jhollay-lal-mandir` and `darya-lal-mandir-darya-lal-sankat-mochan-mandir`
  in Karachi. **"Sorley's parenthesis is an identification the archive does not make, and this chunk
  must not be used to merge the rows… the identification is the index's, it is made in three words,
  it is contradicted by the archive's own row, and it must not be acted on without a human ruling."**
  The two other Sehwan references in that chunk carry no Udero Lal alias, so **the equation rests on
  one parenthesis on one page** plus one index cross-reference. **A human must decide whether it is
  Sorley's error, a genuine nineteenth-century conflation he is reporting, or a reference to a
  Jhulelal cult present at Sehwan; the chunk supplies no basis for deciding, and none should be
  inferred.** The printed form "Jivelal" (p. 257, folio 248) is a third string in the same knot —
  **"Those three rows must NOT be merged with this passage without a human ruling on whether the
  printed 'Jivelal' is the same word as 'Jhulelal' or a text-layer corruption of 'Zinda Pir' /
  'Jinda Pir'."**
- **"Din Belo[text-layer?]" (p. 256, folio 247) and `Din Belo, 247` (p. 434)** against
  **`sadh-belo-sadh-belo-island-temple`** (Sadh Belo Island, near Sukkur/Rohri, Baba Bankhandi
  Maharaj) and **`gurdwara-pehli-patshahi-jind-pir-sukkur`**. **"the chunk prints 'Din', not 'Sadh';
  names no temple, no Baba Bankhandi Maharaj and no Udasi or Nanakpanthi custodianship… Given this
  book's text layer, 'Din' for 'Sadh' is a large corruption, not a small one, so the substitution
  should not be assumed."** Note that chunk 002 writes this row's id as `sadh-belo` while chunks 007,
  009, 011, 013, 016 and 024 write `sadh-belo-sadh-belo-island-temple`, which is the form the index
  supplied to this pass carries; the short form should be treated as a Pass-1 slip, not a second row.
- **"Lahut" against `shah-noorani-shrine-syed-bilawal-shah-noorani`** (Lahoot Lamakan valley,
  Khuzdar District, Balochistan) — raised in chunks 012, 019, 021 and 024. **"Identifying 'Lahut'
  with 'Lahoot Lamakan' is an identification the chunk does not make and would have to come from
  outside the book. NOT MERGED. A human ruling is needed."** Chunk 021 records two details that
  **"make the question live rather than idle"**: the poem puts "a town" on "barren hill" at Lahut
  (pp. 356–357, folios 347–348), and two pages later the holy men "tru�ged[text-layer?] ·
  Kelatwards[text-layer?]" (p. 358, folio 349), "a real direction in Balochistan".
- **"Mian Mir Mahomed" (Kalhora prince) against `shrine-of-mian-mir` (Mian Mir / Mir Mohammad of
  Sind, Alam Ganj, Lahore, d. 1635)** — see the must-not-merge section below.
- **"Shaikh Yusuf Sindhi" (p. 220, folio 211) could be read against `shrine-of-shah-yusaf-gardez`
  (Multan) and `shah-yousuf` (Shahpur, Punjab). "The chunk gives him no place, no tomb and no dates,
  and both rows are in Punjab while the text calls him 'Sindhi'. Left unmapped."**
- **"Makhdum Rukhanaldin" of Tatta (p. 218, folio 209) is NOT Shah Rukn-e-Alam of Multan
  (`shrine-of-shah-rukn-e-alam`), and "Makhdum Miran walad Moulana Yakub" (p. 218, folio 209) is NOT
  Mauj Darya Bukhari of Lahore (`shrine-of-mauj-darya-bukhari`). "no row should be assigned to any of
  these names on resemblance alone."**
- **"Shaikh Khizr", Abul Fazl's ancestor (pp. 219–220, folios 210–211), is not "Khwajo Khizr" /
  "Zinda Pir" (p. 178, folio 169). "Two separate figures sharing a name."**
- **"Saint Udero Lal" in the bibliography — "Tahilram Assudomal. Biography of Saint Udero Lal. 1926."
  (p. 192, folio 183) — could take any of three archive rows and is matched to none: "The chunk calls
  him a 'Saint' but gives no place, no date, no community and no shrine — (not stated)."**
- **"Shah Karim" (p. 212, folio 203) is mapped to `shrine-of-shah-abdul-karim-bulri` only on the
  genealogical link chunk 012 established; "This chunk alone would not carry the identification",
  since it gives neither Bulri nor the durgah.**
- **"Lal Shahbaz" (p. 223, folio 214) is mapped to `lal-shahbaz-qalandar` because the text attaches
  him to Sehwan by name and calls him a saint, but "The book never prints 'Qalandar' and never
  mentions his shrine, ʿurs, dates or order. A reviewer should confirm that Sorley's four grammar
  textbooks are in fact attributed to the saint of Sehwan and not to a homonymous grammarian; the
  attribution of a standard Arabic morphology primer to a thirteenth-century qalandar is the kind of
  claim that a source can get wrong, and the archive should not repeat it without a check."**
- **`shrine-of-sachal-sarmast` is mapped on a person, not a shrine, and "the mapping should be marked
  as weak": "this chunk gives him no tomb, no shrine, no Daraza, no Khairpur, no death year and no
  real name" (p. 299, folio 290); at p. 212 (folio 203, footnote 1) the mapping is "on the name
  alone"; and at p. 192 (folio 183) the name appears only as a book title.**
- **"Hinglaj" (p. 182, folio 173, footnote 1) — chunk 012 declined it for two reasons: the mention is
  inside Gurbuxani's itinerary in a footnote Sorley explicitly disbelieves ("I do not know on what
  authoritv[text-layer?] he holds that Shah Abdul Latif went so far"), and the chunk attaches no
  temple, shrine, deity or observance to the name. Chunk 012 also records that the archive row
  "has no id and so cannot be cited in bold". NOTE FOR THE HUMAN: the `shrine_index.tsv` supplied to
  this pass does carry an id for that row, `shaktipeeth-shri-hinglaj-mata-mandir`. The Pass-1 reason
  for declining Hinglaj was therefore partly an index state that no longer holds; the *evidential*
  reason — Sorley's own disbelief and the absence of any temple or observance — still stands, and the
  decision should be re-taken by a human rather than by a worker.**
- **"Mahars" is not Allo Mahar. "the numerous tribe of Mahars then powerful in Upper Sind" (p. 38,
  printed 29) "must not be mapped to the archive row `allo-mahar` (Allo Mahar, Sialkot), which the
  chunk never mentions."**
- **"Fatehpur" six miles from Larkana (p. 168, folio 159) is not `dargah-fateh-pur-sharif`
  (Gandawah, Jhal Magsi District, Balochistan, Syed Rakhyal Shah, d. 1940).**
- **"Shiv" (p. 363, folio 354) and "Rama"/"Ram" (p. 359, folio 350) are named as objects of an
  ascetic's devotion "with no temple, image, place, festival or custodian attached"; the eight Shiva
  rows and one Rama row are named in the notes so the pages stay findable, and "None is taken."**

### Place-level attachment under ruling (a)

**Ruling (a) is in force and the mapping conversion is another worker's task; what belongs here is
the attachment level, recorded so that a reader of an entry knows what kind of evidence backs it.**
For every toponym in the list below, **the material attaches at PLACE level, not to any particular
site**: the book names the town, the district or the country and says nothing whatever about a
shrine, tomb, saint, ʿurs, custodian or observance there. An entry built on one of these bullets is
citing this book for the tradition of the place, not for its own site.

The places whose material is place-level throughout this book: **Sehwan** (pp. 22, 30, 83, 84, 87,
98, 106, 113, 125, 126, 128, 139, 147, 151, 200; folios 21, 74, 75, 78, 89, 97, 104, 116, 117, 119,
130, 138, 142, 191, and pp. 39, 50, 52 under the spellings "Sewistan", "Seahwaun", "Sewwan",
"Sevensteere (Sehwan)", "Seuestan"); **Sukkur** (pp. 100, 102, 127, 164, 185, 203, 255, 440; folios
91, 93, 118, 155, 176, 194, 246); **Rohri** (pp. 98, 102, 127, 129, 134, 137, 218; folios 89, 93,
118, 120, 125, 128, 209); **Shikarpur** in its five spellings (pp. 20, 62, 66, 100, 101, 103, 124,
126, 127, 137, 164, 190, 200, 440; folios 53, 57, 91, 92, 94, 115, 117, 118, 128, 155, 181, 191);
**Hyderabad** (pp. 20, 100, 101, 103, 123, 125, 126, 127, 136, 138, 170, 190, 191, 202, 435; folios
91, 92, 94, 114, 116, 117, 118, 127, 129, 161, 181, 182, 193); **Karachi** (pp. 20, 100, 103, 109,
125, 126, 127, 136, 158, 159, 170, 190, 191, 225, 436; folios 91, 94, 100, 116, 117, 118, 127, 149,
150, 161, 181, 182, 216); **Khairpur / Khyrpur** (pp. 98, 126, 137, 190, 436; folios 89, 117, 128,
181); **Larkana / Chandookah** (pp. 124, 125, 136, 139, 158, 164, 168, 255, 437; folios 115, 116,
127, 130, 149, 155, 159, 246); **Multan** (pp. 22, 50, 53, 97, 98, 101, 102, 105, 113, 166, 174,
200, 202, 247, 248, 256, 438; folios 88, 89, 92, 93, 96, 104, 157, 165, 191, 193, 238, 239, 247);
**Lahore** (pp. 51, 95, 97, 98, 102, 106, 113, 122, 123, 202, 436; folios 86, 88, 89, 93, 97, 104,
113, 114, 193); **Bahawalpur** (pp. 101, 118; folios 92, 109); **Peshawar** and **Kashmir** (p. 202,
folio 193); **Hala** (p. 182, folio 173; p. 435) and **Matiari** (pp. 167, 218, 225, 437; folios 158,
209, 216); **Umarkot** (pp. 22, 39, 225, 270, 346, 411, 422, 431, 441; folios 216, 261, 337);
**Nagar Parkar / Parkar** (pp. 78, 80, 81, 438; folios 69, 71, 72); **Alor / Alar** (pp. 20, 247,
432; folio 238); **Kelat / Kelatwards** (pp. 39, 358, 431, 436; folio 349); **Lakhi / Lakhiari**
(pp. 137, 151, 167, 436; folios 128, 142, 158); **Lahut / La.hut / Lihut** (pp. 182, 294, 356–363,
431, 436; folios 173, 285, 347–354, 422); **Din Belo** (p. 256, folio 247; p. 434); **Sewistan**
(pp. 119, 173, 439; folios 110, 164). In each case the notes state that no shrine, tomb, saint, ʿurs
or custodian is attached to the name in the passage.

**Two cases are NOT bare toponyms and are recorded differently.** (i) **The Multan proverb** — "a
Persian proverb about Multan … that it consisted of heat, dust, beggars and tombs" (p. 166,
folio 157) — **attaches tombs explicitly to the city while distinguishing none of its rows; the
attachment is to Multan as a place, and "A human should rule on whether a generic 'tombs' reference
to a city is mappable at all."** (ii) **The "tombs, mosques and shrines" phrase** of the three-town
list at p. 218 (folio 209), which attaches an observance to Rohri, Matiari and Tatta at once.

**Person-level attachment.** `bhit-bhit-shah` is the book's dominant row (406 single-id bullets),
but in several chunks the attachment is to **the poet, not to the site**: chunk 004 states that "The
mapping to `bhit-bhit-shah` rests on the running head alone" across pp. 54–66, and chunk 015 that
"'Bhit' appears in this chunk only in the running head … and in the book's title. The
`bhit-bhit-shah` mapping therefore rests entirely on the poet, not on any mention of the site";
chunks 001 and 005 say the same of pp. 1–23 and pp. 67–79. **Since the running head is itself
damaged on nearly every page, and prints `BRIT` on seventy-odd of them, a mapping that rests on the
running head alone rests on a damaged string.**

### Standing must-not-merge flags

- **"Mian Mir Mahomed", the Kalhora prince, must NEVER be merged with `shrine-of-mian-mir` (Mian Mir
  / Mir Mohammad of Sind, Alam Ganj (Dharampura), Lahore, d. 1635, Qadiri).** The flag was raised at
  p. 41 (printed 32), where the book gives "Mian Mir Mahomed, the first prince of that (Kalh6ro)
  dynasty" **only** as a Kalhora ruler who invited the Baluchis in, with nothing of sainthood,
  Lahore, a tomb or a date — **"The names as printed are nearly identical and a consolidator could
  easily merge them… Note also that the chunk's phrase 'Mian Mir Mahomed' sits inside a Burton
  quotation, so it is Burton's spelling, not necessarily Sorley's."** Fourteen later chunks restate
  the flag as untriggered (007–014, 015–023). **It IS triggered in chunk 024:** the index prints
  "Mian Mir Mahomed (Kalhoro) , 32" (p. 437) and "Mian Mir Md . , 32" (p. 436), **"and the index
  makes the trap worse than usual: within two columns it also prints 'Mii: Md . , 54'[text-layer?]
  and 'Mir Md. Khan, 31' in the Kalhora sub-list, and separately 'Mir Muhammad Kalhorn[text-layer?],
  28, 54' and 'Mir Muhammad Khan (Kalhorn[text-layer?]) 3 1[text-layer?]', so at least five Kalhora
  'Mir/Mian Muhammad' names are in play and their page references overlap. Printed p. 32 is the only
  page given for Mian Mir Mahomed."**
- **The two Bumba- brokers must not be merged:** `Bumbae` "(probably Bambomal)", c. 1650 (p. 54,
  folio 45), and `Bumba Riipchand[text-layer?]`, active 1758–60 (p. 66, folio 57). **"A century
  apart."**
- **"Sayid" and "Latif" are not stated to be the same person** (chunk 022): the evidence is
  positional only — "saith the Sayid" (p. 382, folio 373) and "saith Latif" (pp. 386, 390, 397) in
  the identical slot. **"That is an inference, not a statement… A human should confirm it from the
  book's prose before the consolidated entry treats `Sayid.` as Shah Abdul Latif's own voice."** And
  **"'the Sayid' is used for two different people" — as the poet, and as a figure in the Kerbela
  narrative, "The Holy Sayid showed himself, / A hero brave and true" (p. 349, folio 340). "A reader
  coming to p. 349 cold would read the poet into his own poem. Keep the two apart."**
- **"Mir Fateh Ali" (p. 141, folio 132, footnote 2) and "Fateh Ali Talpiir[text-layer?]" (p. 142,
  folio 133) "are not identified with each other by the book" — (not stated) whether they are the
  same person, "though the 1799 work and the 1801 death are two pages apart".**
- **"Umar Siimro[text-layer?]" of Umarkot (pp. 411, 422) and the Sumra dynasty of chunks 012 and 016
  — "Do not merge them on the strength of the spelling; the consolidator must decide, and must note
  that the spellings differ only through text-layer damage."**

### Structure, chapter numbering and apparatus gaps

- **`APPENDIX I` is missing and there is no room for it.** The bibliography ends on printed 186
  (PDF p. 195) and `APPENDIX II` opens on printed 187 (PDF p. 196). **"No page is skipped, so
  Appendix I is not lost between them: it must lie somewhere in PDF pp. 1–188 … or it does not exist.
  This is an unresolved structural gap and a human must find Appendix I before the consolidator
  claims the book's apparatus is complete."**
- **The contents entry for Chapter VI has no page numbers in this text layer** (p. 13), so **"The
  chapter that contains the poet's life is therefore not locatable from this chunk"**; the trailing
  page number for "INDEX TO PARTS J AND II" is also absent.
- **TWO CHAPTERS ARE NUMBERED `CHAPTER VI`** — `A POET AND Hrs[text-layer?] PEOPLE` at p. 172
  (Part I) and `INFLUENCES IN I SLAMIC[text-layer?] MYSTICISM` at p. 284 (Part II). **"A human must
  confirm that against the contents page before any chapter number is cited anywhere in the entries.
  Until then, cite pages only."**
- **"Part" is ambiguous three ways.** Book I / Part I is the economic and institutional history to
  folio 185; Book II / Part II is the poet and the Risalo from folio 208; and the translation opens
  `PART I` at p. 320 (folio 311) and `PART - II` at p. 342 (folio 333). Separately, `PART II /
  LITERARY, CRITICAL AND MISCELLANEOUS` at p. 191 (folio 182) is **a division of the bibliography,
  not of the book** — **"Any consolidator note that says 'Part II begins at p. 191' would be wrong."**
- **The section-number series collides:** Part I of the translation runs I–XXI and Part II runs I–VI,
  so **"this chunk contains two sections numbered `I`, two numbered `II`, and so on to `VI`.
  Additionally `IX` is used three times inside Part I … and `I` is used seven times inside Part II.
  A citation of the form 'section IX' is meaningless in this book."**
- **`BOOK .JI[text-layer?]` (p. 206) — "`II` is the only sensible reading … but the characters as
  extracted are `.JI` and have not been repaired."**
- **The Book II contents page is incompletely numbered** (p. 207): Chapters III, IV and V and their
  sub-entries print no page number at all while the Introduction, I, II, VI (`2 75[text-layer?]`) and
  VII do. **"Either the leaders were lost in the text layer or the printed page genuinely lacks them.
  Check against the PDF before anyone uses this contents page to locate the sur material."** The
  arithmetic conversion of those printed figures to PDF pages is **"an arithmetic consequence of the
  offset, not something this chunk states."**
- **Roman-numeral damage in the section heads and contents:** `(iU)` for (III) (p. 355, folio 346);
  `(VU)` for (VII) (p. 363, folio 354); `(I .I I)` for (III) (p. 366, folio 357); `II . . . THE
  MARTYRS (V) .` (p. 351, folio 342); `VI .   THE SPINNER` (p. 366, folio 357); `XXVlJ`, `XXXVU`,
  `XXXV,UI`, `XXX_I X`, `:X.L`, `XLU`, `HI`, `I I I` (pp. 368–369, folios 359–360); and
  **`{(XXII` for XXXII (p. 389, folio 380) — "the worst-damaged head in the chunk and must be checked
  against the PDF before the section sequence is relied on."** `I II. The .administration of justice`
  (p. 159, folio 150) is **"a damaged section number … carried as printed and not renumbered."**
- **The cycle headings are all four damaged**: `SUHINJ[text-layer?]` (p. 401, folio 392);
  **`MoMuL AND Rl\No[text-layer?]` (p. 411) — "the string `Rl\No` contains a backslash and is the
  worst-damaged heading in the transcribed book so far"**; `LiLAN[text-layer?]` (p. 416);
  `M.ARUI[text-layer?]` (p. 422). **"A heading is exactly the kind of string a later reader will lift
  verbatim; all four must be checked."**
- **The chunk-023 translation section's status "is not stated": "no chapter heading, no appendix
  heading, no part title and no contents line anywhere in the twenty-seven pages… the consolidator
  should not assume either."**
- **The verse appendix's sections I–VI, its own heading, and "any statement it makes about who
  translated the poems, from which text, and on what principles" fall outside chunk 024 (which opens
  at section VII, p. 427, folio 418).**
- **Every chunk is cut at both ends and says so.** The boundary loss that matters most for this
  archive is chunk 010's: **"The list of tax-exempt 'privileged' classes is the chunk's biggest gap…
  the actual enumeration begins in the last printed line of the chunk, 'The exceptions for sar
  shumari were Baliichis, zamindars,' (p. 155, folio 146), and is cut off mid-sentence… Whoever
  consolidates this book must read the continuation at the head of chunk 011 before writing anything
  about religious exemption from taxation in Sind. Nothing may be assumed about sayids, pirs, fakirs
  or shrine endowments from what survives here."** Chunk 011 also warns that Sorley's own note that
  shrine instances "could be multiplied a hundred fold" (p. 168, folio 159) **"warns that this is a
  sample"**.
- **`Appendix VI` (p. 203, folio 194) is the authority for Sorley's retained record spellings and
  Anglicizations and for nothing else** — see the damage inventory above.

### What the Pass-1 workers could not place

- **`Difi[text-layer?]` (p. 72, folio 63)** — "unplaced. No gloss, no footnote."
- **`Qai-a1shhe[text-layer?]` (p. 247, folio 238)** — "unreadable and sits in a list of dynasties…
  Do not use this sentence's list of dynasties without the PDF."
- **`Maskdelites[text-layer?]` (p. 288, folio 279)** — "cannot be identified from the text. No
  expansion or correction has been supplied."
- **`rime�[text-layer?]` (p. 100, folio 91)** — an intensifier, "possibly *far* or *much*", recorded
  as printed.
- **`foundation[text-layer?]` (p. 86, folio 77)** — "reads as though it should be *inundation*", left
  undecided, and complicated because "founded upon the live rock" occurs two pages later in a genuine
  sense.
- **`fled[text-layer?]`, three times (pp. 86, 87, 88; folios 77, 78, 79)** — "may stand for 'fled' or
  'filled' and must not be silently repaired, even though all three read naturally as flight."
- **`places` for `palaces`, twice (p. 123, folio 114)** — "Both read as though the word should be
  *palaces*… but the text layer prints 'places' and it is carried as printed."
- **`fiat` for `flat`, three times (pp. 124, 125; folios 115, 116)** — "flat roofs" prints correctly

- **`warship's[text-layer?]` for "worship's" (p. 363, folio 354)** — "the chunk's most

- **`Suro[text-layer?]` and `idea:s[text-layer?]` in the closing Latin tag (p. 121, folio 112)** —
  "the closing quotation mark is missing and the last word of the tag is damaged. Because this

- **`m built` (p. 49) "is probably 'ill built'" and `fiat bottomed` (p. 51) "probably 'flat
  bottomed', but neither has been repaired here."**
- **`3:` in the dedication poem's third stanza (p. 9)** — "'So now, to contemplative mind / Is.

- **`Haec olim meminisse iuvabit I` (p. 8)** — "ends with a capital I where an exclamation mark

- **Two damaged lines of verse that "should not be quoted at all":** "To be counted in thousand's

  And **"What-No�Existence-Knows[text-layer?] hath gract"[text-layer?]" (p. 254, folio 245) — "Do not

- **"Marni and Marii[text-layer?]" printed as if they were the two parties to a love story (p. 259,

- **The wholly destroyed index line `� fi� �hl ra�   �` (p. 312, folio 303)**, and **"the clause

- **"Barach" (p. 381, folio 372) is unexplained and unrepeated — "whether Barach is Punhun under

- **"Diach the King" (p. 310, folio 301) "is a new name in the book and appears exactly once, in an

### Absences that must not be filled

- **Pages 1–23, 54–66 and 67–79 contain no shrine material whatever** — no shrine, tomb, dargah,
  ʿurs, khanqah, sajjada nashin, endowment, custodian, pilgrimage, building date or saint — and
  chunk 004's only religious word is `Facquirs`, one item in a firman's addressee list (p. 64,
  folio 55). **"Nothing here can support a factual statement about `bhit-bhit-shah` or any other row
  as a site."**
- **Chunk 013 (pp. 189–211): "No sur is named; no manuscript of the Risalo is described; no
  performance, singer, raga, sama or qawwali is mentioned; no ʿurs, fair, mela, langar, offering,
  endowment, waqf, sajjada nashin, custodian, pilgrimage or visitor figure appears; no karamat is
  told; no shrine building or repair is recorded" — all (not stated).**
- **Chunk 014 (pp. 212–226): "no ʿurs, no fair, no offering at a shrine, no custodian, no sajjada
  nashin, no langar, no endowed shrine land, no farmed customs, no tomb-building and no visitor
  figures. Everything institutional in this chunk is about schools, not shrines."**
- **Chunk 015 (pp. 227–242), chunk 020 (pp. 317–347), chunk 022 (pp. 373–399) and chunk 023
  (pp. 400–426) are each recorded as wholly silent on ʿurs, mela, fair, langar, sajjada nashin,
  mutawalli, Auqaf, waqf, endowment, land grant, offering, attendance figure, building date and
  architectural description** — all **(not stated)**, and chunk 022 adds "no tomb, grave, shrine,
  dargah, mazar, khanqah, mosque or ziyarat … no pir, murshid, murid, silsila, order or initiation …
  no place in Sind named at all."
- **No translator is named for a single line of Shah Abdul Latif's verse anywhere in Books II and
  III, while every other poet quoted carries a footnoted translator** (Jami "Translated by Davies",
  p. 276; Abu Sayid via Browne, p. 277; Rumi via Whinfield, p. 291; the Koran "Rodwell's
  Translation", p. 290; al-Kalabadhi "Translated by A. J. Arberry", p. 289). **The seven Risalo
  extracts at pp. 278–280 and 290–291 (folios 269–271, 281–282), the roughly a dozen English verse
  passages at pp. 262–265 (folios 253–256), the thirty-one pages of verse at pp. 317–347 and the
  twenty-seven at pp. 400–426 carry no attribution of any kind — (not stated) — "and must not be
  presented as anyone's published translation. Sorley may be translating himself; the chunk does not
  say so."** The concordance column head reads only "This Tf'anslation.[text-layer?]" (p. 317,
  folio 308) — **"a column head whose whole function is to identify a version, and it identifies no
  person."** Chunk 019 records the one partial exception: **the Preface names Sorley as translator of
  the Muntakhab verse translation (pp. 300, 306, 308), "but it does NOT name a translator for the
  verse quoted in this chunk's own pp. 292–297 (folios 283–288), which belong to Book II… the
  consolidator must not back-attribute them from the Preface, even though it is very likely they are
  his."** The one attributed Book II quotation is `Ramkali` / 'The Holy Men' at p. 300 (folio 291).
- **The Rabia quotation (p. 265, folio 256) is given without a source — (not stated) — and the verse
  beginning "Go, poet, make a song about a fool" is credited only to "initials A.RU." in the Observer
  (p. 261), with no poet named.**
- **No Sindhi, Persian or Arabic script appears anywhere in the book as transcribed.** Every
  indigenous word — `perwannah`, `Phirmaund`, `dastaks`, `chahil-yak`, `kharwar`, `arbaubs`,
  `Kotwal`, `Zamindars`, `Murids`, `dheri`, `kamb6`, `tauhid`, `huliil`, `riih`, `nafs`, `Al-Haqq`,
  `ishk hakkiki`, `ishk maj azi`, and every sur name — is in the book's Roman transliteration, quoted
  as printed. **"The protocol's 'quote the original phrase' rule cannot be satisfied beyond that."**
- **Sorley's own scope disclaimers limit what these pages can be cited for:** "It is no object of
  this book to enlarge on this theme" (p. 110, folio 101) and "Since this `bo0k[text-layer?]` does not
  profess to deal exhaustively with social life … I propose to describe briefly merely some selected
  aspects" (p. 121, folio 112). **"The economic material is Sorley's selection, not a survey."**
- **The gravest single caution in the book, because it governs a whole chapter:** "We have no accounts
  of `Kalhorn[text-layer?]` days. The evidence mostly dates from the time of the `Talpiirs[text-layer?]`"
  (p. 123, folio 114). **"Sorley's descriptions of eighteenth-century Sindhi village life … rest on
  nineteenth-century observers and on his own 1940 observation, by his own admission. No statement
  from pp. 122–125 should enter an archive entry as an eighteenth-century fact without that caveat
  attached."** The same applies to education: "We have Iio[text-layer?] contemporary account"
  (p. 220, folio 211), with the Captain Hart material explicitly dated "1840" and the six madressas
  belonging to Talpur times — **"An entry must not date any of it to Shah Abdul Latif's lifetime."**
- **"Almost every wage and price figure in chunk 009 is imported from outside Sind"** — Masulipatam,
  Surat, Agra, Bombay, Akbar's court, de Boigne's army — on Sorley's stated argument that Sind was no
  different (p. 130, folio 121). **"A consolidator should not present these as Sindhi observations."**
  The genuinely Sindhi figures are Pottinger's livestock prices (p. 129, folio 120), Burton's
  soldiers' pay and Burnes's Lah customs garrison (p. 131, folio 122), and the population estimates
  (pp. 126–127, folios 117–118).
- **Arithmetic the book does not perform must not be performed for it.** "Hamilton in 1699 mentions
  that three years previously … 80,000 weavers perished" (p. 143, folio 134) **"yields 1696, but the
  book prints no year for the plague. Do not write 1696 as if Sorley gives it."**
- **Counts drawn from a single chunk are not counts of the book.** "the count of twenty-three sur
  names is a count of what is printed in pp. 292–316 only and is NOT a count of the Risalo's surs";
  and chunk 022's forty-two Sasui sections — **"Whether these forty-two sections constitute one sur,
  several surs or an editorial selection is (not stated) and must not be guessed."**
- **"Makali" has no archive row and no saint.** The *Tarikh-i-Tahiri* passage at pp. 145–146 is **"the
  richest description of pilgrimage practice the book has yielded"** — a monthly cycle on the first
  Friday and first Monday after the new moon, large parties, self-carried river water and food, and
  "visits to the shrines" in the plural — but **"The book names no saint and no individual shrine at
  Makali: (not stated). A human should decide whether Makali warrants a new row."** So too **Pir
  Murad of Tatta (p. 257, folio 248), Shaikh Patta of Tatta (p. 298, folio 289) — "the chunk's
  strongest candidate for a new one", and "The two are different figures in this book and must not be
  conflated"** — **Adam Shah Kalhoro's tomb at Sukkur (pp. 37–38, printed 28–29)** and **Shahul
  Muhammad Kalhoro's tomb at Fatehpiir near Larkana (p. 168, folio 159).**
- **"The unnamed saint's tomb at p. 267 (folio 258) is deliberately left unmapped. 'A prayer is said
  at the tomb of t;tie[text-layer?] saint' gives no name, no place and no order. It must not be
  attached to `bhit-bhit-shah` or to any other row merely because it stands in a chapter about Shah
  Abdul Latif's poetry."**
